∎

7
Conclusion

We introduced new notions of equivalence between formalisms,
d-weak equivalence
and
d-strong equivalence
, that allow for finer-grained comparisons than weak equivalence. By extending Weir’s idea of control to PDAs, we obtained three new formalisms recognizing
\mathcal{L}_{2}
, all
d-weakly equivalent
to Weir’s original two-level grammar.
But by nuancing the idea of control to account for the difference between CFG derivations and derivation trees, we showed that they are not
d-strongly equivalent
. Instead, three of them are
d-strongly equivalent
to existing
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{L}_{2}}
formalisms, namely TAG, LIG and EPDA, and the fourth is
d-strongly equivalent
to our new
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{L}_{2}}{}
formalism, PAA.

Limitations

We currently give separate definitions of the derivation trees of the four two-level formalisms. Ideally, one could give a general definition of derivation tree in a multi-level formalism, and then derive each particular case by plugging in the definition of derivation trees of (LD-)CFGs and (LD-)PDAs. We leave this generalization for future work.

The
d-strong equivalence
results only hold for spinal TAG and spinal PAA. However, full TAG and PAA are not
d-strongly equivalent
to spinal TAG and spinal PAA, and therefore they are not
d-strongly equivalent
to CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG and CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA, either.

Ethics Statement

The authors foresee no ethical concerns with the research presented in this paper.

Appendix A
Context-Free Formal Systems

A.1
Context-Free Grammars

Definition 27
.

A
context-free grammar
(CFG) is a tuple
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}})
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}
are finite sets of
nonterminal
and
terminal symbols
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
is a finite set of
production rules
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
is a distinguished
start symbol
. The production rules are of the form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{A}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{A}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma})^{*}}
.

Definition 28
.

A
sentential form
of a CFG
G
is a sequence of nonterminal and terminal symbols
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma})^{*}}
.
If
p=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma})^{*}}
, we write
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\xRightarrow{p}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}
.
If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}\xRightarrow{p_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{1}\xRightarrow{p_{2}}\cdots\xRightarrow{p_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{n}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{n}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
,
we say that
G

derives

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{n}
. The language generated by
G
is
\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{s}}\mid\text{${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}$ derives ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{s}}$}\}
.

Definition 29
.

The set of
derivation trees
of a CFG is the smallest set defined as follows.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}
then the tree consisting of a single node
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}
is an
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}
-type derivation tree (whose yield is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}
).

•

If
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{\beta}_{1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{\beta}_{k}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
and, for
i=1,\ldots,k
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{i}
is a
\beta_{i}
-type derivation tree, then the following is an
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}
-type derivation tree:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{k}
\cdots
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1}

whose yield is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Y}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Y}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1})\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Y}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{k})
.

A derivation tree of
G
is an
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}
-type derivation tree.

A.2
Pushdown Automata

Definition 30
.

A
configuration
of a PDA is a pair
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
, where
q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
is the current state and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is the current contents of the stack.

Definition 31
.

If
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})
and
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\beta})
are configurations, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}=(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
is a transition, we write
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}}(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\beta})
.

Definition 32
.

A
run
of a PDA is a sequence of transitions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{n})
such that there are configurations
(q_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}),\ldots,(q_{n},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n})
such that

(q_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{1}}\cdots\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{n}}(q_{n},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n}).

If each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{i}
scans
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}_{i}
, then we say that the run scans
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}_{n}
.

A run
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}
is called a
pop computation
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}
from
q_{0}
to
q_{n}
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n}
, and for all
i<n
,
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{i}|\geq|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}|
.

An
accepting run
(or
derivation
) is a pop computation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}
from
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}
to
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}
.

Definition 33
.

Let
P
be a PDA. If
P
has an accepting run that scans
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{s}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
, we say that
P

accepts

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{s}}
. The language
recognized
by
P
is
\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{s}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}\mid\text{${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}$ accepts ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{s}}$}\}
.

Definition 34
.

The set of
derivation trees
of a PDA is the smallest set defined as follows.

•

The tree consisting of a single node
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon)
(which is the final configuration), is a
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon)
-type derivation tree (whose yield is
\varepsilon
).

•

If
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}})\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}(q^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime})
and
D^{\prime}
is a
(q^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime})
-type derivation tree, then the following is a
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
-type derivation tree:

D={{}{{}{{}}}}\hbox to27.22pt{\vbox to40.17pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 13.6109pt\lower-29.33301pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{{}}{{}{}}
}
\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-13.6109pt}{-29.33301pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{57}\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

If the yield of
D^{\prime}
is
w^{\prime}
, then the yield of
D
is
aw^{\prime}
.

A derivation tree of
P
is a
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}})
-type derivation tree, where
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}})
is the initial configuration.

The definitions of runs, accepting runs, and pop computation for LD-PDAs are analogous to those for PDAs.

Proposition 6
.

Every PDA is
d-strongly equivalent
to a PDA with one state.

Proof.

Given a PDA

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta},(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}),(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon))

construct the PDA

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\{q^{\prime}\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime},(q^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}^{\prime}}),(q^{\prime},\varepsilon))

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{X_{qr}\mid X\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},q,r\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}^{q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}}

and for every transition
(q,X\xrightarrow{a}r,Y_{1}Y_{2}\cdots Y_{k})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
with
k>0
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
contain transitions
(q^{\prime},X^{qs_{k}}\xrightarrow{a}q^{\prime},Y_{1}^{rs_{1}}Y_{2}^{s_{1}s_{2}}\cdots Y_{k}^{s_{k-1}s_{k}})
for all
s_{1},\ldots,s_{k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
.
For every transition
(q,X\xrightarrow{a}r,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
contain transition
q^{\prime},X^{qr}\xrightarrow{a}q^{\prime},\varepsilon
.

We claim that
P
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}^{\prime}
are
d-strongly equivalent
.

(\Rightarrow)
Given a derivation of
P
, apply the above construction to each of the transitions used. Although the construction generates many possible sequences of transitions, exactly one sequence is a well-formed derivation.

(\Leftarrow)
Given a derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}^{\prime}
, just apply the reverse construction to each of the transitions used.
∎

Appendix B
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{L}_{2}}{}
Formalisms

B.1
Embedded Pushdown Automata

To distinguish between the two types of stacks in an EPDA, we will call them the
outer
stack and the
inner
stacks.
A inner stack is written as
[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}]
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}
is written from top to bottom as in a PDA.
We write outer stacks as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}=[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1}]\cdots[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n}]
, where
[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1}]
is at the top, while
[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n}]
is at the bottom.

Definition 35
.

A
configuration
of an EPDA is a pair
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}})
, where
q
is the current state and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
is the current contents of the outer stack.

Definition 36
.

If an EPDA has a transition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}=(q,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}_{1}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}_{2}}})
, then we write, for any
\boldsymbol{\Psi}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}([{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}])^{*}}
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}
,

(q,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\boldsymbol{\beta}]\,\boldsymbol{\Psi})\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}}(r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}_{1}\,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\beta}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}_{2}\,\boldsymbol{\Psi}).

Similarly, if an EPDA has a transition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}=(q,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}r,\varepsilon)
, then we write

(q,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\,\boldsymbol{\Psi})\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}}(r,\boldsymbol{\Psi}).

The definitions of scanning and non-scanning transitions, runs, and accepting runs of an EPDA, and EPDAs accepting strings and recognizing languages, are all analogous to those for PDAs.

Definition 37
.

An EPDA is in
normal form
if its transitions have one of the following types

\displaystyle p,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]\cdots[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\cdots[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}],

\displaystyle p,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}p,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}],

\displaystyle p,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}q,\varepsilon.

B.2
Linear Indexed Grammars

Definition 38
.

A
sentential form

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}]\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma})^{*}}
of a LIG is a sequence of nonterminal symbols, each augmented with a stack, and terminal symbols.

Definition 39
.

If a LIG has a production
p=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\rightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}_{2}}})
, then we write, for any
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}]\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma})^{*}}
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}
,

\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\boldsymbol{\beta}]\,\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}\xRightarrow{p}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\beta}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}_{2}\,\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}.

Similarly, if a LIG has a production
p=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}})
, then we write

\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\,\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}\xRightarrow{p}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}\,\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}.

A LIG derivation starts with the start symbol and initial stack
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]
. The definitions of LIG derivations, LIG derivation trees, LIGs accepting strings, and LIGs recognizing languages are all analogous to those for CFGs.

Definition 40
.

A LIG is in
normal form
if its productions have one of the following types

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{d}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{k}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}],

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}],

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}.

B.3
Tree-Adjoining Grammars

Definition 41
.

A tree
\beta
with a foot node is
adjoined
at an interior node
\eta
by removing the children of
\eta
, replacing
\eta
with
\beta
and inserting the old children of
\eta
as the children of the foot node of
\beta
. The old label of
\eta
is thus lost.

A tree
\alpha
without a foot node is
substituted
at a leaf node
\eta
by replacing
\eta
with
\alpha
. The old label of
\eta
is thus lost.

Definition 42
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit X}\rightarrow\beta)
is a TAG production,
\alpha
is a tree with a node
\eta
labeled
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit X}
, and
\alpha^{\prime}
is the resulting of adjoining or substituting
\beta
at
\eta
, we write
\alpha\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}}\alpha^{\prime}
.

Definition 43
.

Let
G
be a TAG.
If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{1}}\alpha_{1}\Rightarrow\cdots\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{n}}\alpha_{n}

where
\alpha_{n}
does not have any variable nodes, then we say that
G

derives

\alpha_{n}
.

Definition 44
.

The
tree language
of a TAG is the set of trees it derives.
Additionally, the
yield
of a tree is the string obtained by concatenating the terminal symbols at the leaves of the tree, and
the
string language
of a TAG is the set of strings yielded by its tree language.

.

A TAG is in
normal form
if its productions are of the types shown in
fig.

.

:

. Symbols
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}

are constant; other uppercase letters are variables.

B.4
Pushdown Adjoining Automata

Definition 46
.

A PAA is in
normal form
if its transitions are of the types shown in
fig.

Transitions of PAA in

Appendix C
Derivation Trees of Two-Level Formalisms

In
section

5
we showed what the derivation trees of four two-level formalisms look like when both the controller and the controllee are in
normal form
. In this section, we give details on what the derivation trees look like for general controller CFGs/PDAs and controllee LD-CFGs/LD-PDAs.

PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA

The
normal form
and general form of PDAs are not so different, so moving to general PDAs is straightforward. We still distinguish between the cases where the controller transition is (a,b) scanning or (c) non-scanning, and if the former, whether the controllee transition has (a) no distinguished symbol or (b) a distinguished symbol.

(a) If a derivation node is labeled
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[s,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\boldsymbol{\beta}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
,
and
s,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon
is a controller transition,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
is a controllee transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{k}
(with no distinguished symbol),
q,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[s,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}}
{r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{1}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{k}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}

(b) If
s,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is a controller transition,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
is a controllee transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d-1}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d+1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{k}
,
then the node can have child
q,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[s,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\boldsymbol{\beta}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}}
{r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{1}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{d-1}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}[t,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\beta}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{d+1}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{k}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}

Case (c) stays the same.

PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG

Generalizing to a PDA controller is also not very difficult here.

(a) If a derivation node is labeled
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]
,
and
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon
is a controller transition,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
is a controllee production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\rightarrow\upsilon_{1}\cdots\upsilon_{k}
, where each
\upsilon_{i}
is either a terminal or non-distinguished nonterminal symbol,
then the node can have children as follows. For each
i
,

•

If
\upsilon_{i}
is a terminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}
, then there is a child
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}
.

•

If
\upsilon_{i}
is a non-distinguished nonterminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}
, then there is a child
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}_{2}]
.

(b) If a derivation node is labeled
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\boldsymbol{\beta}]
,
and
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is a controller transition,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
is a controllee production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\rightarrow\upsilon_{1}\cdots\upsilon_{k}
, where each
\upsilon_{i}
is either a terminal, nonterminal, or distinguished nonterminal symbol,
then the node can have children as follows. For each
i
,

•

If
\upsilon_{i}
is a terminal or non-distinguished nonterminal, then there is a child as in case (a).

•

If
\upsilon_{i}
is a distinguished nonterminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}
, then there is a child
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}[r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\beta}]
.

Case (c) stays the same.

CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG

Generalizing to a CFG controller is more complicated because a production can now yield multiple terminal symbols, which causes the controllee to use multiple productions in a single derivation step.

Derivation nodes are labeled
\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
is a controller nonterminal, and
X
and
Z
are controllee nonterminals or
\bot
. We write
\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{\bot}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]
.

If a derivation node is labeled
\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{k}
is a controller production, where each
\beta_{i}
is either a nonterminal or a terminal symbol, then the derivation node can have children as follows.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{k-1}}
be any controllee nonterminals.
For each
i
:

(a)

If
\beta_{i}
is a terminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
that names a controllee production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i-1}}\rightarrow\upsilon_{1}\cdots\upsilon_{m}
, where each
\upsilon_{j}
is a terminal or non-distinguished nonterminal (and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i}}=\bot
), then for each
j
,

•

If
\upsilon_{j}
is a terminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}
, then there is a child
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}
.

•

If
\upsilon_{j}
is a non-distinguished nonterminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}
, then there is a child
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]
.

(b)

If
\beta_{i}
is a terminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
that names a controllee production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i-1}}\rightarrow\upsilon_{1}\cdots\upsilon_{m}
, where each
\upsilon_{j}
is a terminal, nonterminal or distinguished nonterminal (and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i}}\neq\bot
), then for each
j
,

•

If
\upsilon_{j}
is a terminal or non-distinguished nonterminal, then there is a child as in case (a).

•

If
\upsilon_{j}
is a distinguished nonterminal, it must be
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{i}}}}
(and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{i}\neq\bot
).

(c)

If
\beta_{i}
is a nonterminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}
,
then there is a child
\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i-1}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}]
.

CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA

Derivation nodes are labeled
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}})
, where
q
is a state and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
is a string of symbols of the form
\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]
(as above, for CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG) or
*
.

If a derivation node is labeled
q,*^{?}\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}

(where
*^{?}
denotes either the presence or absence of a
*
),
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{k}
is a controller production,
where each
\beta_{i}
is either a nonterminal or terminal symbol,
then the derivation node can have children as follows.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{k-1}}
be any controllee nonterminals. For each
i
:

(a)

If
\beta_{i}
is a terminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
that names a controllee transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i-1}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{k}}
(with no distinguished symbol and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i}}=\bot
), then there is a child
{q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{1}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{k}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}

(b)

If
\beta_{i}
is a terminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
that names a controllee transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i-1}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{d-1}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{d+1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{k}}
(and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i}}\neq\bot
), then there is a child
{q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{1}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{d-1}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]*{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{d+1}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{W}_{k}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}

(c)

If
\beta_{i}
is a nonterminal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}
, then there is a child
q,\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i-1}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{i}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}]
\varepsilon

Finally, append
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
to the leftmost child.

Appendix D
D-Weak Equivalence
Proofs

Proposition 7
.

CFG and PDA are
d-weakly equivalent
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}\right)
be a CFG in
normal form
. We define the PDA in
normal form

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}}=\left(\{q\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta},\left(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}\right),\left(q,\varepsilon\right)\right)
, where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}

\displaystyle=\{q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}}q,\varepsilon\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}\}.

We prove by induction on the height
h
of the derivation tree that for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
-type derivation tree
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}
of
G
that yields
w
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
has a pop computation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
that scans
w
.

Base case:
When the height is
h=2
, it must hold that
G
has a production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}
. The yield of the derivation is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}
. By construction,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
has the transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}}q,\varepsilon
, which is a pop computation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
that scans
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}
.

Inductive Step:
We assume that the statement holds for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
-type derivation of height up to
h
and we assume that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}
is a derivation tree of height
h+1
yielding
w=w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{k}
, which has root
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
and children
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{2},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}
. We assume that the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{2},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}
-type derivations have yield
w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{k}
. This implies that
G
has the production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{2}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{k}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
has the transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{2}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{k}}
. By the inductive hypothesis,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
has pop computations (from state
q
to state
q
) of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{2}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{k}}
scanning
w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{k}
, thus it has a pop computation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
that yields
w
.

Therefore, there is a yield-preserving bijection between the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}
-type derivations of
G
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
’s pop computations of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}
(accepting runs).

(\Leftarrow)
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}),(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon))
be a PDA in
normal form
. We define the CFG in
normal form

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}^{\prime})

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}^{pq}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},p,q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}^{q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
is constructed as follows:

•

For each transition
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, and for each
r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
, include production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}_{pr_{k}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}^{qr_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{2}^{r_{1}r_{2}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}^{r_{k-1}r_{k}}
.

•

For each transition
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}}q,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, include production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}^{pq}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}
.

We prove that for each pop computation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
of
P
from
p
to
q
that scans
w
, there is a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}^{pq}
-type derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}
with yield
w
. We prove the statement by induction on the length
l
of the pop computation.

Base case:
When the pop computation has length
l=1
, it must consist of a single transition
p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}}q,\varepsilon
. It scans the string
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}
. By construction,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}}
has a production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}^{pq}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}
, thus it has a derivation yielding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}
.

Inductive Step:
We assume that the statement holds for all pop computations of length up to
l
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}
is a pop computation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}
such that its first transition is
p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{2}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{k}}
, followed by pop computations of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{2}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{k}}
from
p
to
r_{1}
,
r_{1}
to
r_{2}
,
\ldots
, and
r_{k-1}
to
r_{k}
, respectively. We assume that the pop computations of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{2}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{k}}
scan
w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{k}
, therefore
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}
scans
w=w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{k}
. By construction,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}
has a production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}^{pq}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}^{pr_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{2}^{r_{1}r_{2}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}^{r_{k-1}r_{k}}
. By the inductive hypothesis
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}
has
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}^{pr_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{2}^{r_{1}r_{2}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}^{r_{k-1}r_{k}}
-type derivations yielding
w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{k}
, thus it also has a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}^{pr_{k}}
-type derivation yielding
w
.

Therefore, there is a yield-preserving bijection between the accepting runs of
P
(pop computations of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}
from
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}
to
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}
) and derivation trees of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}
.
∎

Proposition 8
.

LD-CFG and LD-PDA are
d-weakly equivalent
, in the sense that
for any LD-CFG
G
, there is an LD-PDA
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
such that for any controller
F
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{F}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{F}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
are
d-weakly equivalent
;
conversely, for any any LD-PDA
P
, there is an LD-PDA
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}
such that for any controller
F
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{F}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{F}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}
are
d-weakly equivalent
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}\right)
be an LD-CFG in
normal form
. We define the LD-PDA

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}}=\left(\{q\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta},\left(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}\right),\left(q,\varepsilon\right)\right)

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
is constructed as follows:

•

For each
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}}}}_{d}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k})
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
, include transition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}}}}_{d}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}
.

•

For each
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
, include transition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}}q,\varepsilon
.

The rest of the proof is similar to the proof of
proposition

7
. The only addition is to observe that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
uses each control word the same number of times that
G
does, ensuring that the derivations of
F
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{F}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{F}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}
are in one-to-one correspondence.

(\Leftarrow)
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},\left(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}\right),\left(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon\right)\right)
be an LD-PDA in
normal form
. We define the LD-CFG in
normal form

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}^{pq}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},p,q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}^{q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
is constructed as follows:

•

For each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}}}}_{d}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, and for each
r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
, include production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}^{pr_{k}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}^{qr_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{2}^{r_{1}r_{2}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}}}}_{d}^{r_{d-1}r_{d}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}^{r_{k-1}r_{k}}
.

•

For each transition
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}}q,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, include production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}^{pq}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit a}
.

Again, the rest of the proof is similar to the proof of
proposition

7
.
∎

Proposition
1
.

CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG, PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG, CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA and PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA are
d-weakly equivalent
.

Proof.

When two two-level formalisms have the same controllee but one has a controller CFG and the other has a controller PDA, it is sufficient to define controllers that are
d-weakly equivalent
. The proof of
proposition

7
shows that this is possible and how the controllers can be defined. If there is a bijection between the derivations of the CFG and PDA for each control word, the rules of the controllee will be applied in the same order in both two-level formalisms, thus generating the same set of strings with an equal number of derivations.

When two two-level formalisms have the same controller but different controllees, the controllees must consume the control words in the same order and the same number of times. Indeed, the proof of
proposition

8
shows that this is possible and how the controllees can be defined.

∎

Appendix E
D-Strong Equivalence
Proofs

We assume for simplicity that the controller CFGs/PDAs and the LD-CFGs/LD-PDAs are in
normal form
in this section. However, the proofs of equivalence also hold in the general case.

Proposition
2
.

EPDA and PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA are
d-strongly equivalent
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)
Let

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta},(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]),(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon)\right)

be an EPDA in
normal form
. Construct the controllee LD-PDA and controller PDA

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{1}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{1},(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}),(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon))

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{2}

\displaystyle=(\{q\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2},(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}),(q,\varepsilon))

where the set of labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}
and the sets of transitions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
are constructed as follows:

•

For each transition
(p,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}p,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}])\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
,
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}
contain a fresh label
\ell
,
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
contain the transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\ell}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
,
and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{1}
contain the transition
\ell\colon p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}
.

•

For each transition
(p,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}r,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]\ldots[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\ldots[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}])
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, add a new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}
, the transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q,\varepsilon
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
and the transition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{1}
.

•

For each transition
(p,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}r,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, add a new label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}
, the transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q,\varepsilon
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
and the transition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}r,\varepsilon
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{1}
.

(\Leftarrow)

We are given
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{2}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{1}
, and by
proposition

6
we can assume without loss of generality that both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{2}
have only one state. Thus

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{1}

\displaystyle=(\{q\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta},(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}_{1}),(q,\varepsilon))

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{2}

\displaystyle=(\{q\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2},(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}_{2}),(q,\varepsilon))

and we construct the EPDA

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\{q\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime},(q,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}_{1}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]]),(q,\varepsilon))

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}\}.

The set
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
is constructed as follows:

•

For each transition
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{k}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
where
k>0
, and for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}_{0}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}_{k}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
contain the transition
q,[\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}_{0}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}_{k}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,[\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}_{0}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}_{1}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{1}}]\cdots\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}_{k-1}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}_{k}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{k}}]\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
.

•

For each transition
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
, and for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
contain the transition
q,[\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
.

•

For each pair of transitions
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
and
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}}}}_{d}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{k}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{1}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
contain the transition
q,[\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{1}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]]\cdots[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\cdots[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{k}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}]]
(where the
[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
is in the
d
-th position).

•

For each pair of transitions
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
and
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}q,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{1}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
contain the transition
q,[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]]\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}q,\varepsilon
. ∎

Proposition
3
.

LIG and PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG are
d-strongly equivalent
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}\right)
be a LIG in
normal form
. Construct the controllee LD-CFG and controller PDA

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}\right)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\{q\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2},\left(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}\right),\left(q,\varepsilon\right)\right)

The set of labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}
, the set of productions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}_{1}
and the set of transitions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
are constructed as follows:

•

For each production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\rightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]{}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
contains the transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
.

•

For each production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\rightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{1}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]\cdots}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\hbox{}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]{\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{k}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]}}
, add a fresh label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}
, the transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q,\varepsilon
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
and the production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}}}}_{d}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{k}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}_{1}
.

•

For each production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}}
, add a fresh label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}
, the transition
q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q,\varepsilon
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
and the production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{a}}
to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}_{1}
.

(\Leftarrow)
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{1}=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}\right)
be a controllee LD-CFG and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{2}=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2},\left(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}\right),\left(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon\right)\right)
a controller PDA, both in
normal form
. Construct the LIG

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}\right)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}^{q}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\}

and the set
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
is defined as follows:

•

For each transition
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
, and for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
contain a production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}^{p}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\rightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}^{q}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]{}}
.

•

For each transition
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q,\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
and production
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\breve{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}}}}_{d}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{k})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}_{1}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
contain a production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}^{p}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\rightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{1}^{q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]\cdots}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}^{q}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\hbox{}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]{\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}_{k}^{q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}]}}
.

•

For each transition
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit\ell}}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{2}
and production

contain a production

. ∎

is a CFG and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{1}
is an LD-PDA. Without loss of generality, we may assume by
proposition

6
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{1}
has only one state, so

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{P}}_{1}

\displaystyle=(\{q\},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}_{1},(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}}_{1}),(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon))

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{G}}_{2}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S}_{2})

We further assume that both are in normal form.
Then we construct the PAA

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},\mathcal{V},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}_{1}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit S_{2}}])

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle=\{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}]\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}\}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
is constructed as follows:

•

For each production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B}_{k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}_{2}
, where
k>0
,
and for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}}_{k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
,
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{\prime}
contain a transition
